台新證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市仁愛路四段 118 號 16 樓(02)5589-9558  (113) 金管投顧新字第 021 號 
公司拜訪快報

南亞(1303)：電子材料獲利與業外轉投資貢獻優於預期 
建議：維持買進

一、3Q26 自結獲利優於預期 
3Q26 營收季增 14.3%至 955.97 億元， 加上電子材料獲
利大幅成長，以及業外南亞科及台塑化挹注，單季自結
EPS 達 4.90 元、優於預期；累計前三季 EPS 為 10.07
元。各部門營益率中，電子材料自 2Q26 的 19.9%跳升
至 30.9%，塑膠加工則為 5.4%、化工部門 0.6%、聚酯
部門 0.2%。 化工與聚酯部門獲利衰退主要是2Q26 有低
價庫存，塑膠加工則因通膨干擾終端消費。然而，EG
在中國華東港口庫存嚴重偏低下，近一季報價及利差表
現好轉，抵銷可塑劑產品與 BPA 的虧損，聚酯部門則有
美國外銷動能挹注，支撐兩個部門 3Q26 小幅獲利。展
望 4Q26， 終端消費依舊沒有明顯的動能 ， 但EG 報價及
利差持穩且聚酯朝電子應用發展，管理層預期化工、聚
酯部門營益率與 3Q26 相當，電子材料在漲價延續及產
品組合優化下，營利率有望持續走升。

二、2027 年營運動能依舊為電子材料與南亞科貢獻 
3Q26 電子材料營收達 555 億元，其中，CCL 營收比重
達 31%、PCB(南電) 29%， 銅箔與Epoxy 則各佔 11%、
玻纖 7%。 獲利結構來看 ， 玻纖營益率走升至40%以上，
CCL 則季增 10 個百分點至 30%並高於 PCB 的 26%，
銅箔受惠加工費調漲達 15%，Epoxy 則維持 10~11%左
右。2027 年電子材料依舊是成長主力，部門營收比重將
達 60%以上，營益率有望在單季接近 40%的水準，但材
料漲價趨勢會從玻纖轉為 CCL。轉投資方面，南亞科因
記憶體市場供需缺口急遽放大 ， 預估將貢獻2026、2027
年稅前 EPS 9.17 與 16.74 元。

三、高階 CCL 訂單自 2H26 逐步出貨，2027 年效益將
明顯放大 
2025 年南亞 CCL 單價約每張 500 元，3Q26 已走升至
1,000~1,100 元/張，主要受惠漲價與產品組合優化。管
理層預期 4Q26 維持每月漲福 5~10%，且有機會延續至
1Q27。3Q26 中下旬 M 系列在 CCL 的營收比重由 20%
提升至 30%，漲價及產品組合優化都有影響，1Q27 在
M6、M7 訂單放量下會有更明顯的增長。目前 M4 CCL
單價約 1,100~1,200 元/張，M6 約當每張 2,200~2,300
元，M7 單價則跳升至 4,000 元以上 ， 有助2027 年 ASP
漲幅進一步擴大。此外，3Q26 的 CCL 產能利用率達
90~95%，現階段 CCL 月產能 618 萬張，規劃樹林、新
港將進行產線整改，預計 2026 年底跟明年可以各增加
約 15 萬張/月。 
四、維持買進理由 
3Q26 自結獲利優於預期，電子材料漲價與 CCL 產品組
合優化支撐本業中長期動能，轉投資南亞科(2408)與台
塑化(6505)大幅挹注業外收益 ， 上修2026、2027 年 EPS
分別為 15.72 及 29.24 元。考量獲利顯著成長有助股息
提高，且市場因公司獲利結構轉型而 Re-rating，維持買
進評等，目標價調升至 450 元(20x 2027 本業 EPS+10x 
2027 南亞科 EPS)。

金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 9 月 30 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 美國企業投資動能維持韌性 ：8 月標普全球美國綜合 PMI 升至 58.4，創2021 年 7 月來高點 ，
製造業、服務業同步擴張至 57 和 58.7； 8 月扣除飛機與國防的核心資本財訂單月增 1.6%，
高於前月修正後的 0.6%。其中，初級金屬、機械及電機設備訂單均呈現成長，顯示企業資本
支出、AI 投資與國防需求仍具韌性，持續支撐美國經濟成長。
◼ 美中同意貿易休兵再延長兩個月：美中同意調降雙邊總值 600 億美元商品的關稅，涵蓋美國

玉米、化妝品及中國家電、玩具等產品，並延長貿易休兵至明年 1 月 10 日，同時中國承諾繼
續供應稀土並擴大採購美國農產品與煤礦，美國則繼續暫停實施 50%穿透性規則；AI 方面，
美中雙方雖未就 AI 監管議題達成共識 ， 但同意建立風險溝通機制 ， 並於11 月底前展開對話 。
◼ Meta 旗下 AI 代理人 Muse 登頂排行榜：Meta 新款個人 AI 代理 Muse 上線後迅速累積逾
250 萬次下載，並登上美國 App Store 下載排行榜首位。相較過去生成式 AI 以問答與內容生
成為主，Muse 可進一步協助安排日程、搜尋資訊、處理文件及完成交易，代表 AI 正從提升
效率工具逐步走向數位勞動力，市場評價焦點也將逐漸由 AI 基礎設施擴散至應用軟體業者。
市場展望
◼ AI 需求持續擴張，科技供應鏈成長動能延續：AI 基礎建設需求持續擴張，帶動 HBM、伺服器
DRAM、 先進製程與封裝等關鍵供應鏈維持成長動能 ， 市場表現由全面上漲逐步轉向結構性分
化。若 AI 伺服器需求持續成長且 CSP 資本支出維持高檔，具供給優勢、定價能力及較高訂單

能見度之企業，其營運表現有機會獲得支撐。整體而言，科技產業獲利表現仍具關注度，惟後
續仍須留意全球景氣、產業供需變化、政策監管及市場評價調整等風險因素。
◼ 債券佈局可關注中天期美國公債及投等債：中天期美國公債及投資級債仍值得持續關注：**雖
然貨幣政策風險下降有助於穩定長期通膨預期 ， 但政府與企業發債供給增加所帶來的排擠效應 ，
以及美國經濟成長展現韌性，仍可能使長天期利率面臨上行壓力。因此，在當前利率環境下，
中天期債券於票息收益與存續期間風險之間相對較為均衡。投資級債方面，彭博美國投資級公
司債指數利差約 80 個基點，雖然利差偏窄，但高基準利率環境仍提供相對較高的收益水準。
因此，在目前市場環境下，中天期美國公債及投資級債仍為值得持續觀察的債券資產類別，惟
後續表現仍須留意利率走勢、信用利差變化及經濟成長動能等因素。

### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 澳洲央行決議如預期升息一碼至 4.60%為今年第四度升息，基準利率創 2011 年
以來新高，澳洲央行在政策聲明中表示通膨壓力尚未緩解，不排除在11、12 月
政策會議進一步升息；另一方面，澳洲8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)月增 0.4%略低
於預期，但年增 4.0%仍高於澳洲央行 2~3%的目標區間。
⚫ 美國第二季美 GDP 終值從 1.5%上修至 2.2%， 其中實質個人消費支出創下2025
年 3 月以來最大單月增幅，顯示即使高利率持續，美國家庭消費依然具韌性；
另一方面，美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增月增 0.2%、3.0%，雙雙低於市場預
期的 0.3%、3.3%，美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬人低於預期的 9 萬人，失
業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，8 月非農就業人數亦同步下修至 13.3 萬人，通膨下滑
和勞動力市場略有降溫，降低市場對聯準會 10 月升息預期，Fedwatch 顯示 10

月升息機率下降至 20%左右。
⚫ 受美伊衝突引發的能源價格衝擊影響 ，9 月歐元地區消費者物價調和指數(HICP)
整體上升速度遠超預期，其中，法國年增 3.4%、德國年增 3.3%，歐元區年增
3.8%，均高於歐洲央行的 2%目標，反映能源價格的衝擊持續影響物價，加深歐
洲央行再次升息的壓力。
⚫ 日本上週公佈貨幣政策會議紀要及央行意見摘要顯示多名委員認為當前政策利
率依然處於中性區間之下 ，需警惕通膨上行風險 ， 且未來應該以更快速度升息抑
制通膨，然而意見摘要提及日本經濟財政大臣城內實表示希望央行充分審視過
去升息對景氣之影響 ，市場解讀為政府牽制日本央行未來升息步伐 ， 而財務大臣
片山皋月則重申尊重日本央行的自主性。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/5/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.760，低點在 31.946，以 31.830 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 5 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶，週初，國際油價持穩於每桶 100 美元上方，市場預期聯準
會後續仍有升息的空間，提振美元指數表現，加以外資賣超台股並匯出，台幣走貶
至 31.900 上方；不過適逢月底也是季底，匯價逢高吸引出口商進場賣美金，緩解台
幣貶值的速度；臨近週末，美國 8 月核心 PCE 數據 3%低於預期 3.3%，但市場等待
9 月非農就業數據，交投偏向謹慎觀望，最終在央行調節之下收在 31.830。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬
人，低於市場預期 9 萬人，且失業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，顯示就業市場動能略有
減弱，市場對於 10 月升息的預期下降，但仍預期 12 月會再度升息。美元指數雖然
脫離高點，但仍在 102 附近高位震盪，預計將給台幣帶來壓力；就台灣市場來看，
台股再創收盤新高且外資轉為買超台股，且隨著 AI 需求持續帶動台灣經濟出口表
現強勁，預計出口商後續拋匯需求將持續提供台幣支撐。綜合以上，預估本週台幣
區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.600-32.000

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/5/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪偏升。隨著美中兩國完成新一輪談判，雙方同意延長休兵，從
原定的 11 月延長到明年 1 月 ， 降低短期貿易摩擦升溫的風險 ， 支撐人民幣的走勢由
6.7170 走升至 6.7030；不過由於美伊戰事持續 ，國際油價持穩於每桶 100 美元上方 ，
加以美元指數及美債收益率雙雙走高 ， 令人民幣由升轉貶至6.7200 上方 ； 臨近週末，
美國公布 9 月非農數據降溫，聯準會10 月升息預期下滑，美元指數回落，境外人民
幣走升至 6.7000 週內高點，終場境外人民幣收在 6.7061，週線升值 166 點，境內人
民幣於十一長假前夕收盤於在 6.7053，週線升值 72 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週人民幣區間震盪 。 就國際市場來看 ， 美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬
人，遠低於市場預期 9 萬人，且失業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，顯示就業市場動能略
為減弱。數據公布後，市場對於聯準會10 月升息的預期下降，美元指數小幅回落，
有助於人民幣的表現；就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價格再度升破 6.7400，有助
於人民幣的走勢 ， 不過10/1~10/7 是中國十一長假 ， 預計人民幣走勢主要將受國際美
元影響；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，各天期均線仍向下發散，空頭格
局不變，不過匯價反彈至 20 日均線附近，顯示下行動能暫緩。綜合以上，預估本週
人民幣區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6900-6.7300

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Oct/5/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY 震盪上行。週初，美元指數持穩在 101 以上高位，USD/JPY 大致
在 156.50 至 157.85 間波動；週間，適逢日本上半年會計年度結算，出口商拋匯使資
金匯回力道強勁 ， 帶動USD/JPY 下行最低來到 156.35，但逢低有買盤進場，USD/JPY
回到 157 整數關卡之上，後隨美債殖利率創高、美元指數突破 102 關卡，USD/JPY
上行最高來至 158.45；臨近週末，美國公布 9 月非農就業人數低於預期，USD/JPY
一度回落至 157 下方，終場收在 157.83，週線上漲 57 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪 ， 主要原因如下 ： 一 、日本 9 月製造業 PMI 由 54.9
回落至 54.1，增長速度放慢至六個月以來的低位，主因為產出和新訂單增長放緩，
經濟復甦力道可能減緩，不利日圓表現。二、美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增 3.0%，
美國 9 月非農數據 2.9 萬人均低於預期，通膨和勞動力市場略有降溫使市場對聯準
會升息預期下滑，加之部分聯準會官員表示無需急於進一步升息，Fedwatch 顯示 10
月升息機率降至兩成，然而美國第二季 GDP 上修至 2.2%經濟基本面穩健仍將支撐
美元。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY 匯價處於區間波動，且各天期均線
趨平，上方 158.50 有年線、季線壓力，下方 156.25 則有月線支撐，預期短線以區間
震盪可能性較高。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：155.50-159.00

美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Oct/5/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，美元指數持穩在 101 以上高位，EUR/USD 震盪下
行至 1.1310，而在紐約聯儲主席威廉姆斯發表不急於再次升息的偏鴿言論後，
EUR/USD 反彈至 1.1340；週間，美國 8 月 PCE 數據低於預期，EUR/USD 一度反彈
至 1.1380，然而在全球債券拋售潮下，資金回流美元帶動美元指數突破 102 關卡，
EUR/USD 跌破 1.1300 關卡最低來至 1.1214；臨近週末，美國 9 月非農就業人數低
於預期，EUR/USD 反彈至 1.1285 後回落，終場收在 1.1253，週線下跌 138 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
預估本週 EUR/USD 震盪偏弱，主要原因如下：一、上週公佈歐元地區消費者
物價調和指數(HICP)，法國年增 3.4%、德國年增 3.3%、歐元區年增 3.8%，顯示能
源價格衝擊依然使通膨壓力居高不下， 市場預期歐洲央行可能在 12 月再度升息 ， 將
帶給歐元部分支撐。二、美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增 3.0%、月增 0.2%低於預
期，而 9 月非農就業人數 2.9 萬人低於預期的 9 萬人 ， 失業率則由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，
就業降溫及通膨回落降低市場對聯準會十月升息的預期，然而第二季美國 GDP 終
值從 1.5%上修至 2.2%，經濟基本面持穩仍有利美元維持強勢。三、技術面而言，在
日線圖中，EUR/USD 匯價在各天期均線下方並跌破 1.1300 整數關卡，月線向下穿
過季線形成死亡交叉，顯示短線空方動能較強。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈
現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1150-1.1400 
前週交易區間 
1.1214 1.1391

                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115.09.29-115.10.02 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.65% ~ 1.68% 
公債－10 年期-A15109     1.95% 
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.82%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ， 本週為提存期底 ， 繼央行上週大砍存單效應持續發酵 ， 市
場資金供給仍充裕，惟需求疲弱，帶動短率朝區間低位移動。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.66%~1.68%，利率高位較上週下跌 1 個基本點。 
二、 本週市場交投仍冷清，5 年期指標券 A15108 週三於處所成交在 1.82%，10 年
期指標券則未成交，週五等殖系統參考報價中價為 1.95%。 
第四季台債發行量雖偏少 ， 惟美伊和談依舊陷入僵局 ， 國際油價續處高位 ， 通
膨疑慮尚存，國際債市走勢仍偏空，預期台債殖利率短線將維持高檔盤整格
局，預估 5 年券區間為 1.85%~1.90%，10 年券區間為 1.95%~2%。

市場焦點
美國
科技股
• 美國企業獲利維持強勁 ，背後主要受經濟展望穩健及 AI資本支出熱潮支撐 。從獲利來看 ，市
場共識預期標普500企業EPS成長率2026年達28%，2027年及2028年則分別為20%及16%，
雖呈現逐步降溫 ，但仍維持雙位數增長 。標普500預期本益比已由約 23倍降至19倍，且並非
主要來自股價修正 ，而是企業獲利預期上調所致 ，在企業獲利維持成長 、AI投資持續挹注及
估值已較高點收斂的情況下，標普500仍具中長期配置價值。
美債
外匯對沖視角
• 在全球公債遭遇拋售潮與通膨陰影下 ，美債因利率期間結構與匯率優勢 ，今年以美元計價總
回報優於多數G7主權債，儘管海外私人機構投資人正在等待長端利率落底訊號以重返市場 ，
對美元資金或以美元計價為主的投資人而言 ，當前美債名目殖利率仍處於數十年高位 ，具備

相當良好的長期鎖利價值，但價格波動風險考量下，建議債券佈局仍以中短天期為主。
台股
• 8月景氣對策燈號續亮紅燈，綜合判斷分數41分，較前月持平，對應的燈號仍是代表景氣熱絡
的紅燈，連續第9個月亮出紅燈，追平史上最長「連紅」紀錄，顯示國內經濟維持成長。
• 根據歷史統計第四季台股上漲的機率接近八成 ，除了AI投資趨勢未減 ，年底集團作帳行情以
及美國11月期中選後不確定性下降有望支撐台股多頭情緒 。群益投顧樂觀看待台股第四季表
現，有望上看53,000點。此外，彭博綜合統計台灣加權指數12個月目標價達59,688點。

就業增幅明顯放緩，降低Fed短期再升息急迫性
• 非農明顯降溫，勞動市場尚未出現全面惡化：9月非農僅增2.9萬人，遠低於預期，且7、8月合計下修6萬人；失業率升
至4.2%，但勞參率同步升至 61.8%，家戶調查就業增加 40.6萬人，顯示失業率上升部分來自勞動供給增加 ，其中營建、
製造業仍分別增加1.1萬及0.9萬人，平均工時維持34.4小時，數據呈現招聘動能明顯放慢，但尚未出現全面性就業惡化。
• 薪資增速放緩，Fed短期政策壓力下降：9月平均時薪月增僅0.1%、年增3.0%，均低於前值；同時8月PCE年增3.4%、
核心PCE年增3.0%，顯示薪資通膨壓力有所緩和 ，但整體物價仍高於Fed 2%目標，在9月Fed升息1碼後，非農報告降
低Fed在10月連續升息的必要性，市場焦點轉向年底通膨與AI投資驅動的產業動能。

• 投資建議：短期關注美國中短天期投資級債券 ，就業降溫與薪資放緩有助降低短端利率上行壓力 ，但通膨仍高於目標 ，
因此不宜過度 預期長天期利率快速回落；股市則可續關注 AI基礎建設與資本支出相關產業 ，因營建及製造業就業仍具
韌性，顯示企業投資動能尚未明顯轉弱。

消費動能回升、通膨低於預期，內需韌性與物價壓力並存
• PCE低於預期、通膨降溫仍待確認：美國8月PCE物價指數月增0.3%、年增3.4%，核心PCE月增0.2%、年增3.0%，均低於
市場預期；其中核心PCE較修訂後7月的3.3%下降，顯示物價壓力有所緩解，但仍高於Fed 2%目標，此次年度更新回溯修
訂2021年以來資料，亦使歷史基期重新調整，後續仍須觀察通膨下降是否能延續。
• 消費回升、所得增幅偏緩：8月個人所得月增0.2%，個人消費支出（PCE）月增0.9%、實質消費成長0.6%，顯示內需仍具
韌性；惟消費增幅明顯高於所得 ，個人儲蓄率由7月4.6%降至4.1%，為2022年11月以來低點，反映家庭可支應消費的財
務緩衝持續縮小，後續消費動能仍須觀察。
• 後市觀察：PCE低於預期後，市場對10月再升息的預期明顯降溫，但長端美債殖利率仍受通膨、財政供給與期限溢價牽動

而維持高檔；建議可聚焦獲利成長與現金流穩健的優質大型成長股與短天期美債以掌握收益 ，同時留意儲蓄率下滑與 9月
消費者信心低迷可能抑制後續消費動能，動態調整部位並嚴控風險。
資料來源：U.S. Bureau of Economic Analysis， 2026.10.1

能源與AI推升長端利率，融資成本壓力延續
• 長端殖利率上行 、期限溢價成重要推力 ：近期
美債呈現短端與長端走勢分化 ，雖市場對 10月
Fed再升息預期因 8月PCE低於預期而明顯降溫 ，
10年期及30年期的美債殖利率仍偏高位 ，反映
通膨風險 、財政融資需求與期限溢價上升 ，長
天期無風險利率維持高檔 ，並推升房貸及企業
融資成本。
• 能源與AI投資推升通膨及資金需求 ：油價維持
高檔增加通膨黏著風險 ，AI基礎建設投資則帶
動企業資本支出與融資需求 ，兩者共同提高長
期資金需求；在財政赤字與美債供給仍大的環
境下，長端殖利率可能維持較高水準 ，進而提
高股票估值折現率及高槓桿企業的融資壓力。
• 後市觀察： 短期持續觀察油價 、10年及30年期
美債殖利率與期限溢價變化；債券配置仍建議

降低長天期債券部位以降低利率風險 ，優先布
局短天期高品質債券獲取穩健息收 ，股票則聚
焦具穩健現金流 、低財務槓桿與定價能力的企
業，以降低高利率環境下的估值與融資風險。

AI需求大增，美光財測超過市場預期
• AI記憶體需求強勁，美光財測顯示景氣延續：美光科技9/30盤後公布財報，2026財年的第四季營收達542.3億美元，年增

379%，調整後EPS 33.42美元，均優於市場預期；公司並預估2027會計年度第一季營收615億美元、調整後EPS 38.15美元，
分別高於市場預期的567.7億美元及36.02美元。強勁財測反映AI資料中心持續推升HBM、伺服器DRAM及高速SSD需求。
隨Amazon、Alphabet等大型科技企業持續擴大AI基礎建設投資，記憶體需求仍具成長動能。
• 長約與產品升級，支撐中長期業績：美光目前已簽署36份多年期供應協議，涵蓋約三分之一營收並延伸至2030年，有助降
低記憶體產業週期波動；同時持續布局HBM4、HBM4E及高容量DDR5等產品。公司2026會計年度自由現金流達590億美元，
並預期2027、2028會計年度供需將進一步緊俏。整體而言，AI需求已由單一HBM擴展至伺服器記憶體與儲存產品，美光財
測亦顯示景氣延續性仍強，後續市場焦點將轉向擴產速度、資本支出及AI需求成長能否維持。
資料來源：美光，彭博，鉅亨網，2026.10.1

• AI驅動獲利成長，企業基本面仍具支撐：美國企業獲利維持強勁，背後主要受經濟展望穩健及AI資本支出熱潮支撐。從獲利
來看，市場共識預期標普500企業EPS成長率2026年達28%，2027年及2028年則分別為20%及16%，雖呈現逐步降溫，但
仍維持雙位數增長。其中，AI基礎建設與大型雲端業者仍是重要驅動來源，2026年對EPS成長的貢獻合計約19%，2027、
2028年仍分別貢獻14%及11%。
• 獲利上修降低估值壓力：標普500預期本益比已由約23倍降至19倍，且並非主要來自股價修正，而是企業獲利預期上調所致。
雖然未來EPS成長率若由高檔回落，市場仍可能面臨評價修正壓力，但在企業獲利維持成長、AI投資持續挹注及估值已較高
點收斂的情況下，標普500基本面仍具支撐。因此，短期應留意獲利增速放緩與市場持倉偏高所帶來的波動，但從獲利成長

與估值兩大面向觀察，標普500仍具中長期配置價值。

中國製造業PMI(%) 企業利潤前瞻指數反彈上行(%)
BCI:企業利潤前瞻指數 BCI:企業銷售前瞻指數
2026年製造業PMI 2024年 2025年 2023年
生產回升與價格上漲同步推動中國PMI改善
• 生產回升與價格上漲同步推動 中國PMI改善： 9月製造業PMI升至50.1%，較前月回升0.3個百分點，重返榮枯線以上，顯示
製造業景氣有所改善 ，主要受生產活動回暖及價格上漲帶動 ，中小企業景氣同步改善 ，但仍低於榮枯線，大型企業表現相對
較佳，高技術製造、裝備製造維持擴張 ，消費品PMI升至50.7%，重回景氣區間 ，另一方面，原材料及能源價格上漲推升企
業成本，上下游獲利差距持續擴大 。 非製造業PMI則由49.0%升至50.2%，服務業受節慶效應帶動回溫 ，建築業則受財政資

金投入，及基建建設推進而改善，不過需求復甦仍偏溫和，企業成本壓力尚未明顯減輕。
• 結論：第四季隨著基建投資、財政支出及房市支持政策逐步落地，經濟可望獲得支撐，但10月受長假因素影響，PMI可能短
期承壓。

台灣景氣燈號連9紅 台股Q4有望突破5萬點大關
• 8月景氣對策燈號續亮紅燈： 綜合判斷分數41分，較前月持平，對應的燈號仍是代表景氣熱絡的紅燈 ，連續第9個月亮出紅
燈，追平史上最長「連紅」紀錄，顯示國內經濟維持成長。國發會表示，AI需求令台廠積極擴廠，加上政府推動AI新十大建
設，民間投資有望維持動能。此外，受惠財富效應、企業調薪及就業市場穩定，民間消費有望持續成長。
• 法人看好台股年底前站上5萬點：根據歷史統計第四季台股上漲的機率接近八成 ，除了AI投資趨勢未減，年底集團作帳行情
以及美國11月期中選後不確定性下降有望支撐台股多頭情緒 。群益投顧樂觀看待台股第四季表現 ，有望上看53,000點。此
外，彭博綜合統計台灣加權指數12個月目標價達59,688點。
• 觀點：近期台股站上48,000點整數關卡，總市值達5.3兆美元，讓台股耀升成為全球第四大市值股市 ，顯示台股在AI浪潮下

的得天獨厚。年底前仍須留意中東衝突進展，若能源通膨壓力持續，聯準會升息預期恐為股市帶來波動，但考慮AI科技持續
發酵，建議投資人續抱台股，並以定期不定額的策略紀律加碼，目標追求中長期完整的AI趨勢回報。
資料來源：國發會，2026.09.29。

外匯對沖視角—美債對沖後殖利率屬全球最低之一
• 美國國債殖利率真的高嗎？在當前全球總體經濟環境下，美國國債殖利率雖維持在數十年歷史高點附近，但若單憑名目殖利
率來判斷美債的吸引力，容易忽視匯率對沖與各國財政風險帶來的溢價差異。
• 美元視角下的殖利率比較： 若將其他已開發市場的主權債務 (如德債、日債、英債)完全對沖回美元，美債在殖利率曲線上多
數期限的實際殖利率，其實與德債並列全球最低水準。相反地，日本國債與英國國債經匯率對沖回美元後的收益率反而更高。
• 跨貨幣資產交換 (ASW)的真實回報： 以10年期主權債的對沖跨貨幣資產交換計算：美國 10年期國債有效殖利率約 4.85%，
日本10年期國債(對沖至美元)有效殖利率約5.06%，德國10年期國債(對沖至美元)有效殖利率約4.51%，這表明美國政府發

債的真實對沖融資成本其實已相對精實，且符合國際市場對各國財政基本面的合理定價。
對沖至美元後的10年期主權債殖利率
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.29

財政基本面與信用風險—殖利率溢價反映國家債務比重
• 從歐元計價或信用風險的角度來比較 ，美債的定價邏輯非常符合總體經濟基本面： 儘管債務以歐元計價時 ，10年期美債殖
利率比同期限德國國債高出約30個基點，這反映出德國的債務佔GDP比重僅約65%，而美國則高達127%，因此美債承擔了
適度的財政溢價。儘管義大利的債務佔GDP比重高達140%且信用前景較遜色，但在統一貨幣基準下，義大利國債殖利率仍
較美國國債高出60個基點，這顯示市場並未過度壓低美債信用，美債依然享有優質避險資產的溢價優勢。
• 海外私人投資者的資金流向：近一年海外私人機構在美債加碼速度放緩，乃通膨環境下全球其他資產選擇增加，及過去升息
波段導致帳面按市值計價虧損 。然而歐洲投資人(持有一半以上的海外美債)目前仍持有龐大部位，並在等待適當的加碼時機。

• 小結與投資建議：在經過外匯對沖後，美債收益率在發達國家主權債中處於相對低位，發行成本合理且具競爭力。美債殖利
率高於德債低於義債 ，精確地反映美國政府債務負擔率 (127%)與總體經濟穩健度 。在全球公債遭遇拋售潮與通膨陰影下 ，
美債因利率期間結構與匯率優勢 ，今年以美元計價總回報優於多數 G7主權債，儘管海外私人機構投資人正在等待長端利率
落底訊號以重返市場，對美元資金或以美元計價為主的投資人而言，當前美債名目殖利率仍處於數十年高位，具備相當良好
的長期鎖利價值，但價格波動風險考量下，建議債券佈局仍以中短天期為主。
歐元計價美債殖利率高於德但低於義

亞洲晶⽚債券跑輸指數，評級保持穩健
• 亞洲晶⽚債券跑輸指數，評級保持穩健：儘管AI熱潮推動收⼊創下紀錄 ，但SK海力⼠和台積電的美元債 ，今年以來的表現仍
略遜於彭博亞洲美元信用債總回報指數 ，主因為大規模資本支出擴張 ，以及潛在的投⼊資本回報率下降令 其利差承壓，美國
雲服務巨頭創紀錄的 AI支出計畫加劇了壓力 ，儘管如此 SK 海力⼠和台積電仍保持較強的高評級信用實力 ，台積電計畫投
⼊600-640億美元資本支出 ，SK海力⼠的資本支出則處於 40兆韓圜區間。兩家公司穩健的資產負債表 、合計共1,260億美元
的淨現金、強勁的經營性現金流，以及在先進製程，和HBM晶⽚領域的市場領先地位，為上述支出提供支撐。
• 投資建議：現階段台積電與SK海力⼠美元債最大的問題不是信用風險 ，而是估值與利差壓力 ，短期可能因AI資本支出過大，

及市場情緒波動而表現落後指數 ，但以兩家公司強大的現金流 、淨現金部位及產業領導地位來看 ，信用品質仍屬亞洲投資級
債中的優質標的，建議利差擴大時分批布局 。

顧 • 企業獲利成長將支撐股價上行：標普500指數的長期走勢與企業每股盈餘（EPS）高度連動。高盛預估，標普500企業EPS將
於2026年達375美元，並在2027年進一步升至415美元，對應年增率約36%與11%。在利率維持高檔、金融條件偏緊的環境
下，企業獲利仍持續處於成長軌道，將為股市提供穩健的中期上行動能。
• 本益比回落提供潛在布局契機：根據FactSet數據，標普500企業第三季整體獲利預計成長30.6%。其中，資訊科技類股獲利
成長預估高達66.5%，領先能源以外的其他產業；同時，該類股預估本益比已由今年高點回落，在獲利上修與估值修正同步
發生的背景下，浮現具吸引力的中期布局機會。
• 小結：短期市場仍可能受地緣政治與利率預期擾動，但中期趨勢仍由企業獲利擴張所主導。建議投資人採取分批布局科技股

策略，聚焦具備AI受益結構、資本支出能見度高及現金流穩健的龍頭企業。

1. 本資料內容僅供內部同仁及客戶參考，非作為任何金融商品或服務之購買或銷售之推介，不得視為買賣有價證券或其他金融商品之要約或要約之引誘。
2. 本資料提及之經濟走勢預測不必然代表基金之績效，基金投資風險請詳閱基金公開說明書。
3. 基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益 ，投資人申購
前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。

### Page 2

Taiwan Electronics & Semiconductors
12 October 2026 Citi Research
2
PCB and substrate- 3Q26 was a strong quarter for substrate and CCL sectors, with
demand continuously piling up. Unimicron posted +23% QoQ sales growth in 3Q26,
beating Citi/consensus forecasts by 4%/6%. We expect 4Q26E ABF sales
momentum to be even stronger vs 3Q considering all the AI chips ramping up
together. CCL names also saw strong momentum across the board thanks to
enhanced product mix and benefit from price hike in 2Q26. We expect CCL sector to

see another round of price hikes from Nov onwards. For PCB, GCE’s Sept sales were
impacted by flooding in Thailand, which led to logistics disruption, while Tripod
posted record monthly sales backed by memory demand and early pull-in before
China Golden Week. With TPCA Show coming next week, we think
substrate/CCL/PCB sectors might see some event catalysts in the near term.
However, from a fundamental perspective, we still prefer substrate
(Unimicron>NYPCB>Kinsus) > CCL (EMC> TUC) > PCB.

PC/NB Supply chain - For NB ODMs, overall NB shipments see MoM increase in Sep
(+32% MoM) due to the quarter end inventory build; combined 3Q NB shipments
were down 17% QoQ due to  the early pull-in in 1H. We forecast 4Q26E NB shipments
to further decline -7% QoQ/-28% YoY given the muted demand on rising retails
prices and the increasing constraint in components procurement, While the price
hike effect in the NB products should partially offset shipment decline, we expect

increasing margins dilution impact to the NB ODM supply chains.
For lens components - Largan reported relatively muted sales results in Sep at
NT$5,779m (+15% MoM/-7% YoY); combined 3Q revenues are below market
estimates while Genius’ 3Q revenues were slightly better than expectation. Despite
the near-term sales performance, we see more upside opportunity coming from
their new development in optical business in the longer term. We expect Genius’
project with a leading US company on optical connector and ferrule to start

shipment in 1H27. For Largan, we expect it to benefit most from FA/FAU for CPO
market. We see its current engagement with the leading US client progressing
smoothly, as evidenced by its continued new land expansion for future capacity
build-out (see our recent report).
Thermal – Sep-26 sales as reported by thermal players (AVC & Auras) were up from
Aug at +6% MoM/+42% YoY. While AVC delivered another record month thanks to
solid ASIC demand and initial Vera Rubin shipments, Auras experienced MoM

decline due to client deferring pull-ins and Thailand flooding. Going forward, we
preliminarily expect both companies to see sequential revenue growth in 4Q26E,
driven by the AWS Trainium transition from air to liquid cooling, ramp-up of the TPU
and Vera Rubin projects.
Power – Delta reported MoM revenue recovery in Sep-26 (+1% MoM/+14% YoY), with
power electronics down 4% MoM. 3Q26 revenues were up 8% QoQ but below our
and Street’s expectation. We expect Delta to regain momentum with double-digit

QoQ growth in 4Q26E as ±400V enters the initial transition period, while in the long
term, HVDC is still progressing gradually while Delta maintains its technology
leadership. For BBU, we see Sep sales showing mixed momentum yet resilient BBU
demand remains intact. Dynapack’s Sep-26 sales declined 34% y/y and 54% m/m
to NT$776mn, reflecting 1) weaker PC shipments amid quarter-end inventory
adjustments and 2) deferred shipments of one BBU product, broadly consistent with

the near-term weakness flagged in our Aug-26 sales note. We expect a meaningful
sales rebound from Oct helped by shipments of new higher-power BBU products.
Meanwhile, AES maintained solid momentum with Sep-26 sales rising 25% y/y (+1%
m/m), suggesting resilient underlying BBU demand. Similarly, STL (4931.TWO; not
covered) delivered robust 176% y/y growth, further reinforcing our positive view on
the sector. We retain our constructive stance on BBU names, supported by

sustained demand and upcoming product ramps
Passive components - Yageo reported trended up monthly sales in Sep-26, as
demand in Europe and US recovered after the summer holiday, on top of sustained
AI demand. 3Q26 revenues were slightly above cons forecasts. Current quarter top
line still beats mgmt. 's "moderate growth" guidance. We believe that growth was
again broad-based across commodity and specialty products, and driven by both
volume and price. We expect a more visible ASP uplift in 4Q26E as specialty and

contract-based business could slow vs the spot and distributor channels.

institutional clients and the management teams of covered companies. Typically, company management is more likely to
participate when the analyst has a positive view of the company.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

Key Takeaways from Company Meetings

Key Takeaways from Applied Materials Meeting 
Participants: Kevin Moraes, VP , Strategy and Marketing; Sree Kesapragada, VP , 
Advanced Packaging; Mike Sullivan, Head of Investor Relations 
Investor questions mainly focused on clarifications tied to the company’s investor event 
and financial targets. On revenue and capital intensity, management noted that 
semiconductor industry pricing is a key variable that it cannot control - but has high 
confidence in its framework tied to increasing WFE intensity per node - and these 
increases have already been programmatically locked in by customers today. On 
Applied’s target of 200bps of market share gain, management noted the complex set of 
mix effects involved in the calculation, but noted the substantial impact from China 
export control exclusions. Relative to the company’s newly articulated gross margin 
target of 54% by 2030, Applied believes there is the potential for upside to this target - 
but pointed out that the vast majority of its costs are variable in nature. On its optical 
tool portfolio, Applied believes that it can leverage its expertise and market position in 
e-beam technology to expand into this adjacent $9bn market. On AGS, management has 
high confidence in its outlook for mid-teens long term revenue growth - but noted that 
its services attach rate is higher on its newer tools relative to its installed fleet. 
Key Takeaways from Lam Research Meeting 
Participants: Ram Ganesh - VP of Investor Relations 
Consistent with prior commentary, Lam expects 8-10 new fab projects to come online in 
2027 - and currently expects product growth to be driven by DRAM, followed by 
advanced foundry/logic, followed by NAND flash. The company has not specified a 
timeframe for achieving its new long-term gross margin target of ~55%. However, we 
expect this to be achieved over several years, and see it tied more to product mix 
(particularly with new products such as Akara) and overseas operational manufacturing 
efficiencies rather than like-for-like pricing. Management remains constructive on the 
company’s competitive positioning in Logic, DRAM, and advanced packaging - 
particularly as etch and deposition intensity continues to grow with a variety of new 
device structures coming into the mix - including hard masks, surface prep, TSVs, and 
other materials engineering steps. 
Key Takeaways from Teradyne Meeting 
Participants: Roy Chorev - VP & GM of Compute Test Division; Amy McAndrews - VP of 
Investor Relations 
Teradyne announced a strategic partnership with and investment in FormFactor at 
SEMICON West. Teradyne remains committed to an open ecosystem of probe card 
vendors, but believes Form’s strong position in silicon photonics warrants a closer 
relationship that enables Teradyne to commit additional engineering platform resources. 
Regarding its XPU business, Teradyne continues to expect to gain merchant GPU share in 
the long run - and noted that Rubin test times are nearly 2X Blackwell given the near 
doubling in transistor count. Teradyne is also pursuing the tester opportunity at Intel, 
which is focused on increasing its exposure to external vendors (currently using an 
14 October 2026   2
Goldman Sachs Americas Technology: SemiconductorsFor the exclusive use of KEVINLU@LENOVO.COM
e92c7a75ab8b4efbba794e6b187208c8

### Page 3

internal solution) beginning with its foundry business, where Teradyne sees a
medium-term opportunity. In its memory business, Teradyne expects minimal impact
from customer de-specing of HBM as its demand is tied primarily to unit volume rather
than stack height. In co-packaged optics, Teradyne continues to see a very strong
product ramp opportunity, although given the early stages of development, there are
likely to be significant shifts between testing intensity at different insertions during the

manufacturing process.
Key Takeaways from Camtek Investor Meeting
Participants: Lior Aviram - Executive Chairman; Rafi Amit - CEO; Ramy Langer -
President & COO; Moshe Eisenberg - CFO; Eyal Segev - Marketing Director
We attended Camtek’s Investor Meeting at SEMICON. Our key takeaways are as follows:
Long-term financial targets: In conjunction with its investor meeting, Camtek n
pre-announced 3Q26 revenue of $160 mn –$161 mn (above its prior guidance),

guided to ~$180 mn for 4Q26, and provided an initial 2027 revenue outlook of $800
mn based on orders received to date. Management laid out a long-term revenue
target of $1.2 billion in about three years, with a gross margin target of 55%-56%
and operating margin target of 34%-36%.
Advanced packaging remains the key growth driver: Camtek stressed that n
advanced packaging remains its core business, accounting for ~80% of recent
orders, and expects advanced packaging wafer growth to run at about twice the

broader industry rate through 2031 (13% vs. 7% CAGR). Within advanced packaging,
the company expects the high-end of the market (which accounts for the vast
majority of its business) to grow at a ~24% CAGR through 2031, driven by AI, silicon
photonics, denser interconnects, and increasingly complex inspection and metrology
requirements.
Technology and product roadmap centered on inspection and specialized n
metrology: Camtek’s roadmap remains centered on its margin-accretive Hawk and

Eagle products for inspection (~85% of its 2026 mix), as well as its metrology
platforms. Separately, management also noted it is embedding AI into detection and
classification and plans to launch AI-enabled software in 2027 (which it attributed to
its Visual Layer acquisition).
M&A strategy focused on capability acquisition and strategic expansion: Camtek n
noted that it is pursuing both smaller acquisitions that add technology and/or talent
to its core business, as well as larger strategic transactions that broaden its offering,

while emphasizing that its $1.2 bn revenue target and financial model do not assume
meaningful M&A activity.
Key Takeaways from Onto Innovation Meeting
Participants: Brian Roberts - CFO; Sidney Ho - VP of Investor Relations
Onto remains very constructive on the demand outlook through 2H26 and heading into
2027 across a range of demand drivers - with growth likely to accelerate next year given
the growth of incremental market opportunities. In HBM, Onto believes its business is

likely to be minimally impacted by any “de-spec” activity by XPU vendors, as its tool
14 October 2026   3
Goldman Sachs Americas Technology: SemiconductorsFor the exclusive use of KEVINLU@LENOVO.COM
e92c7a75ab8b4efbba794e6b187208c8

### Page 4

demand is far more driven by unit volume than stack height. The company continues to
see CoWoS as a key demand driver that is increasingly driven by OSAT partners rather
than TSMC; Onto sees EMIB-T as a significant opportunity that promises to be material in
2028. From a competitive standpoint, Onto remains highly confident in its market share
position, including at TSMC - and sees no imminent impact from potential product
launches by competitors. On margins, management remains confident in its target of

350bps of gross margin expansion, which it believes can be achieved solely by product
mix and efficiencies in its manufacturing operations. The company believes it has room
to grow revenue substantially from projected levels, and does not see itself as capacity
constrained.
Key Takeaways from MKS Instruments Meeting
Participants: John Lee - President & CEO; Paretosh Misra - VP of Investor Relations
MKS believes the industry is in the early-to-middle stages of a substantial cyclical

industry upturn driven by AI and datacenter demand. The company is currently
preparing for a potential WFE spending envelope of up to $300bn in 2028, and
potentially higher over time. In the near-term, management expects the company to
potentially outgrow its customers (2Q26 was the first time the company delivered Y/Y
Semiconductor revenue growth in excess of them) given supply chain inventory effects.
MKS believes it has enough capacity to support WFE of ~$250bn with its Penang facility

(with a surge capacity of another 20%), but may need to expand capacity further if
these forecasts come to fruition. Importantly, the company does not believe it is
constraining its customers’ growth plans today. MKS has not taken significant pricing
actions, but could do so selectively in order to capture the value it delivers. Management
believes its product portfolio is well positioned, but would consider a possible
divestiture of its specialty industrial product lines at an attractive valuation.

### Page 5

Valuation/Risks

AMAT (Buy, on CL): Our 12-month price target of $670 is based on a 32X P/E multiple 
applied to our normalized EPS estimate of $21.00. Key downside risks include: (1) 
incremental export restrictions; (2) local China-based supplier share gains. 
LRCX (Buy): Our price target is $380, which is based on a 35X multiple applied to our 
normalized EPS forecast of $10.75. Key downside risks: (1) softer DRAM/HBM demand; 
(2) delays in planned clean room and fab expansions; (3) lower-than-expected content 
gains from advanced packaging and 3D architectures. 
TER (Buy): Our 12-month price target is $465, which is based on a 37X P/E multiple 
applied to our normalized EPS forecast of $12.50. Key downside risks: (1) delays in the 
revenue ramp with a merchant GPU customer, (2) significant quarterly volatility due to 
the lumpy nature of large AI-related infrastructure projects, (3) slower-than-expected 
recovery in Mobile. 
ONTO (Buy): Our12-month price target is $400, which is based on 32X our normalized 
EPS estimate of $12.50. Our target P/E is in line with peer multiples, and it is on the 
higher end of ONTO’s historical median, given recent momentum around HPC. Key 
upside/downside risks: (1) additional volume purchase agreements and accelerated 
customer adoption of Dragonfly G5; (2) shift towards OSATs or domestic Chinese 
manufacturers; (3) competitor launching a rival tool. 
CAMT (Neutral): Our 12-m price target of $160 is based on a 38X P/E multiple applied 
to our normalized EPS of $4.20. Key upside/downside risks: (1) notable push-outs in 
chiplet architectures; (2) failure to pass on higher pricing; (3) better-than-expected 
growth in HPC-driven revenue in the medium-term. 
MKSI (Sell): Our 12-month price target is $270, which is based on a 20X P/E multiple 
applied to our normalized EPS of $13.50. Key upside risks: (1) rebound in the NAND 
market from an equipment ordering perspective; (2) greater realization of synergies 
from the Atotech acquisition; (3) any material pickup in momentum for the company’s 
Semiconductor business due to broader WFE trends.

### Page 6

Wiwynn (6669.TW)
19 October 2026 Citi Research
6
Wiwynn
Company description
Established in April 2012 and listed in March 2019, Wiwynn is one of the
leading cloud infrastructure providers focusing on servers, storage, and rack
solutions for global CSPs' data centers. Key customers include Microsoft,
Meta Platforms, and Amazon, which together accounted for >90% of sales
with tier-2 CSPs accounting for single-digit percentage of sales. Wiwynn
used to be running at an asset-light business model but has expanded

capacity for server motherboard manufacturing and rack final assembly on its
own in recent years, increasing in-house supply ratio.
Investment strategy
We rate Wiwynn as Buy. We expect the secular growth in global data center
and cloud-based IP traffic to act as a key driver for Wiwynn in years to come.
We expect Wiwynn to benefit from data center growth amid rising ODM
Direct/white-box server adoption. With order momentum having started to
recover since 4Q23, we see Wiwynn's growth being backed by continuous

rollouts of data centers from existing customers and new project wins from
Amazon.
Valuation
Our target price for Wiwynn is NT$3,200. We value Wiwynn based on PE as
we believe the company’s earnings performance and industry dynamics will
be the key focus areas for the market. Our TP is based on 16x 2027E EPS,
which is at the top end of the historical trading range since listing and above
the average PE multiple of the peer group. We believe our target multiple is

justified by the company’s 100% exposure to data centers, rising scale, and
robust earnings growth, as well as stabilizing ROE.
Risks
Key downside risks that could cause Wiwynn’s shares to trade below our
target price include: 1) Potential volatility in capex deployment; 2) Component
price hike; 3) Peer competition; and 4) FX volatility.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

Background: Zhen Ding Technology (ZDT), the world’s largest PCB 
manufacturer by revenue, generated revenue of TWD182.5bn in 2025. 
Historically focused on consumer electronics and smartphones, the 
company is shifting towards higher-value AI-server PCBs (HLC/iHDI), 
optical-module PCBs (mSAP), and IC substrates (ABF/BT). These 
businesses accounted for 21.6% of its revenue in 1H26, up from 11.7% in 
2025. Management targets this ratio to reach c.25% in 2026 and c.30% in 
2027. To support this shift, ZDT has 22 fabs under construction and plans 
to build nine more, primarily for mSAP/HDI, HLC, and ABF substrates.

Highlights: ZDT’s “One ZDT strategy” is gradually bearing fruit. Since 
2018, management has pursued its “One ZDT strategy” to broaden the 
product portfolio – including HLC, substrates, mSAP/HDI, rigid-flex and 
SLP – by leveraging its strengths in FPCB and consumer HDI. This 
strategy is beginning to pay off, and management expects its fast-growing 
segments, including mSAP HDI for optical transceivers, AI-server HDI and 
ABF/BT substrates, to gain momentum from this year. Management 
expects revenue from these segments to increase by c.150% YoY and 
account for c.25% of total revenue (or c.TWD50bn) in 2026, and to further 
increase by 100% YoY to TWD100bn in 2027. ZDT targets these fast-
growing segments to reach 30% of its revenue mix in 2027 and c.45-50% 
in 2030. Given current progress, the 2030 target could be reached in 2028. 
For mSAP HDI, management expects the 800G/1.6T transceiver product 
mix to shift to 7:3 in 2027, from 5:5 in 2026, and aims to become the largest 
supplier of optical-transceiver HDI in 2027. In AI-server HDI, we expect the 
2 leading ASIC vendors to gradually shift from pure HLC to thick HDI 
boards – a combination of HLC and HDI – in their next-generation designs, 
with ZDT a potential beneficiary. This could drive c.3x growth in ZDT’s 
business in 2027.

Product mix to support gross margin expansion. Margin expansion is 
increasingly driven by a favourable product mix. Gross margin reached 
22.4% in 1H26 (vs. 16.5% in 1H25). Management indicated that optical-
transceiver HDIs currently have the highest gross margin, at over 40%, 
followed by IC substrates, with ABF margins potentially reaching 50-60% 
and BT margins around 30% as a long-term target. Server HLC margins 
are around 20-30%, while HDI margins should be higher given their greater 
process complexity. With margins in traditional businesses at c.20%, ZDT 
expects growth in the newer segments to drive further margin expansion.

Valuation: The stock is trading at 36/19x PER for 2026/27E based on 
Bloomberg consensus. It’s past 3-year PER range is 10-30x.

20 October 2026

Business transformation bears fruit

➢ Key beneficiary of growth in optical transceiver & AI server HDI demand  
➢ Aggressive substrate capacity strategy paying off in current conditions   
➢ Margin expansion on fast-growing segments (HDI and substrates)

水冷機櫃與機殼本業同步走升 
 2Q26 本業穩健成長 ，EPS 1.41 元：晟銘電 2Q26 營收 32.19 億元，
年增 22.5%，毛利率則從前期 21.1%降至 20.5%，主要是因客戶
Storage 相關產品出貨增加，然 Storage 產品已進入成熟階段，客戶
持續每季議價，2Q26 稅後淨利為 2.9 億元，EPS 1.41 元。 
 預期 3Q26 為營運谷底，4Q26 開始爬升：展望 26H2，3Q26 營收年
增 16%，估計 3Q26 稅後 EPS 1.49 元。4Q 通常為傳統旺季，加上
VR200 也會在 4Q26 陸續加入量產，而晟銘電也有參與其中，出貨
量將逐步上升 ， 隨著 VR200 及 ASIC 等新平台陸續進入放量階段，
預期機殼與 Sidecar 出貨量將同步提升。此外，2026 年 ODM 專案
有 8 至 9 成聚焦機櫃業務，涵蓋 OCP、EIA 以及 MGX，預期 L11
系統整合業務營收將進一步提升。 
 維持買進，目標價 140 元：投顧認為晟銘電 4Q26 起受惠 VR200 機
殼與 ASIC 放量，2027 年營運可望進一步成長，且公司已成功切入
Meta 供應鏈，並在冷卻機櫃與機構件取得關鍵認證，可直接承接
Meta 釋出的巨額硬體訂單，且在液冷架構成為主流帶動下，晟銘電
水冷相關業務比重持續提升 ， 使2026 年整體毛利率有望升到 20%以
上 ， 投顧預估2026 年 EPS 6.13 元，2027 年 EPS 9.4 元 ， 維持買進 ，
目標價 140 元。

個股報告

晟銘電 為伺服器機殼大廠，目前
切入 IT 機櫃、水冷機櫃、水冷搭
配的分歧管 
 晟銘電子科技股份有限公司成立於 1976 年 6 月 ， 為台灣伺服器機殼OEM/ODM
製造商， 公司主要銷售電腦機殼及伺服器機殼 ，其用途為桌上型電腦及伺服器等
各項組件之安裝防護設備 ， 近年來公司積極跨足散熱領域並研發設計出液冷散熱
的浸沒式散熱產品。於 2024 年公司推出多款液冷機櫃及液冷散熱系統。晟銘電
提供全球客戶迅速且彈性的 OEM 及 ODM 生產服務。2026 年 1~8 月主要產品
營收占比，伺服器 73%、L11 系統整合 18%、Tooling 6%、DT 3%。

Exhibit 3: 產品組合

Sources : Company、MasterLink

2Q26 本業穩健成長，EPS 1.41
元 
 晟銘電 2Q26 營收 32.19 億元，年增 22.5%，毛利率則從前期 21.1%降至
20.5%，主要是因客戶 Storage 相關產品出貨增加，然 Storage 產品已進入成熟
階段，客戶持續每季議價，2Q26 稅後淨利為 2.9 億元，EPS 1.41 元。

預期 3Q26 為營運谷底，4Q26
開始爬升 
 展望 26H2，3Q26 營收年增 16%，估計 3Q26 稅後 EPS 1.49 元。4Q 通常為
傳統旺季，加上 VR200 也會在 4Q26 陸續加入量產，而晟銘電也有參與其中，
出貨量將逐步上升，隨著 VR200 及 ASIC 等新平台陸續進入放量階段，預期
機殼與 Sidecar 出貨量將同步提升。此外，2026 年 ODM 專案有 8 至 9 成聚焦
機櫃業務，涵蓋 OCP、EIA 以及 MGX，預期 L11 系統整合業務營收將進一步
提升。

今年前八月 L11 系統組裝營收占比已達 18％ ， 而明年L11 系統組裝營收貢獻還
會持續提升。公司在機櫃產品線已相當完整，還可提供配套的水冷配件，機箱
基本上是跟隨著輝達的腳步走 ， 包括GB300、VR200 等標準品機箱都已經開好
模具。海外產能布局，公司目前生產基地仍以中國大陸為主，其中東莞為主要
生產基地，另於寧波設有產能，產品以機殼、機櫃為主，東莞廠自動烤漆線預
計今年底前完成，一天可以完成60 個機櫃烤漆；台灣中壢則主要負責打樣及水
冷產品研發，部分零組件仍由中國生產後運送至中壢進行後段組裝。泰國一期
廠已完成建置並具備生產條件 ， 二期則持續進行設備調校 ， 主要導入烤漆產線，
預計 4Q26 開始試產，後續待客戶完成稽核及認證後逐步量產。泰國二期的烤

漆設備規格除可支援機殼外，亦可延伸至機櫃產品，有助未來將部分機櫃製程
移往海外。今年中壢廠、泰國廠都有新產能加入，而明年資本支出大致也會維
持在大約 7～8 億元。

維持買進，目標價 140 元 
 投顧認為晟銘電 4Q26 起受惠 VR200 機殼與 ASIC 放量，2027 年營運可望進
一步成長 ， 且公司已成功切入Meta 供應鏈 ， 並在冷卻機櫃與機構件取得關鍵認
證，可直接承接Meta 釋出的巨額硬體訂單，且在液冷架構成為主流帶動下，晟
銘電水冷相關業務比重持續提升，使 2026 年整體毛利率有望升到 20%以上，
投顧預估 2026 年 EPS 6. 13 元，2027 年 EPS 9.4 元，維持買進，目標價 140
元。(15x2027EPS)

2Q26 營收 18.24 億元， +5%QOQ，+12%YOY，2Q26 毛利 率
25.87%，低於元富預估的 26.5%，主要是中國地區仍有低價競爭影
響，本業獲利達成率 99%，業外收益挹注下，2Q26 EPS 2.02 元。 
 交期拉長，2027 年預計擴產 40%，2027 年營收挑戰 100 億元:載板
客戶積極擴產，載板三雄欣興、南電、景碩均為公司客戶，近年積極
布局轉戰載板市場的臻鼎亦為客戶，由於載板面積增大， 技術難度提
升，為了維持相同生產效率須提高自動化資本支出，故以過往來看，
自動化設備佔客戶資本之約為 5%，現在提升至 15%。晶圓製造及
OSAT 隨客戶台灣及海外積極擴產， 交期過去 4-6 個月現在已經拉長
到 6-8 個月，載板、晶圓客戶、OSAT 客戶均樂觀，2027 年營收挑
戰 100 億元， 預計2027 年將新增產能 40%，以支應客戶未來需求。 
 維持買進評等，目標價 220 元：3Q26~2027 年營收逐季增長，新產
能逐步開出， 故維持買進，預估2026 年 EPS 8.51 元，2027 年 EPS 
11.40 元(原估 10.95 元)，目標價 220 元。(2027 PER 20x)

迅得為自動化設備供應商 
 迅得為自動化設備供應商 
迅得機械成立於 1999 年，主要產品為印刷電路板、軟板、光電及半導體物流系
統之自動化解決方案，2025 年營收比重可區分為 PCB 事業部(41%)、半導體-
載板事業部 13%、半導體-晶圓事業部 12%、半導體-封裝事業部 32%，面板事
業部 2%。主要客戶包括欣興、南亞、景碩、台郡、臻鼎、日月光、台積電、友
達、群創等。

個股報告

載板 、 晶圓客戶 、OSAT 客戶均樂
觀，2027 年營收挑戰 100 億元

載板、晶圓客戶、OSAT 客戶均樂觀，2027 年營收挑戰 100 億元 
迅得 1H26 營收占比為 PCB 4 1%，載板 16%，晶圓製造 16%，封裝 25%，其
他 2%。PCB 主要產品以自動上下料、物件搬運及智慧倉儲為主； IC 載板亦為
自動上下料較多，整廠規畫約佔比 60%，單機搬運 20%，倉儲 15~20%；半導
體晶圓廠則以倉儲為主，台灣部分標準倉儲較多，微型倉儲較少， 海外部分目
前未出貨標準倉儲，僅供應微型倉儲，主要是安裝時間及人力配置考量；封裝
OSAT 廠主要供應倉儲佔比 60~70%，自動化搬運約 30%。

載板客戶積極擴產，載板三雄欣興、南電、景碩均為公司客戶，近年積極布局
轉戰載板市場的臻鼎亦為客戶，以欣興資本支出來看，2026 年 340 億元，2027
年長交期採購設備訂單金額 92 億元 ， 均將投入桃園楊梅廠及光復廠 ； 景碩2026
年資本支出 140 億元(原本是 80 億元)，將投入於六廠建置及設備導入，2027
年資本支出為 160 億元(原本是 100 億元)， 擴產進度明確 。 由於載板面積增大，
技術難度提升 ，為了維持相同生產效率須提高自動化資本支出 ，故以過往來看，
自動化設備佔客戶資本之約為 5%，現在提升至 15%。

晶圓製造部分，隨 客戶台灣及海外積極擴產，因目前現有自動化廠商均無單一
廠商可獨立供足客戶所需 自動化設備量體，故每個案件 公司均有相對應配比，
以海外廠來說，考量人力成本及安裝時間公司僅提供微型倉儲為主。 OSAT 部
分，客戶拉貨持續積極，半導體業務維持樂觀。以交期來看，過去 4~6 個月現
在已經拉長到 6-8 個月，載板、晶圓客戶、OSAT 客戶均樂觀，2027 年營收挑
戰 100 億元，預計 2027 年將新增產能 40%，以支應客戶未來需求。

維持買進，目標價 220 元

維持買進評等，目標價 220 元 
3Q26~2027 年營收逐季增長 ， 新產能逐步開出 ， 故維持買進 ， 預估2026 年 EPS 
8.51 元，2027 年 EPS 11.40 元(原估 10.95 元)， 目標價220 元。(2027 PER 20x)

風險提示 
出貨不如預期，ASP 不如預期。

LG에너지솔루션 (373220)

Company Brief 
이차전지/석유화학 
Analyst 노우호 
wooho.rho@meritz.co.kr 
RA 황현식 
hyeonsig.hwang@meritz.co.kr Long Trading 구간  
3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후: ESS ↑, BEV ↓  
LG에너지솔루션의 3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후 배터리 섹터는 11/3 미국 중간선거
를 전후로 Trading Long 투자의견이 유효하다. 미국 중간선거 이후 특정정당 우세와 
별개로, 미국 Ex-China ESS 공급망 프리미엄은 유효하다. 동사 역시 그간의 ESS 
사업 난제(단위당 수율, 팩 병목)는 점진적 해소될 점이다. 반면 시장의 BEV 반등 기
대감에 동의하지 않는다. EU 프랑스와 독일의 내연기관 퇴출시점 지연 움직임 및 미
국 연비규제 정책 폐지 결정으로 전통 완성차 고객사들향 BEV 배터리 출하 부진은 
여전하겠다. 미국의 BEV 정책 변화시점은 28.11월 대선으로 지연될 전망이다.

ESS 모멘텀: 한국 제3차 ESS 중앙계약시장 사업자 선정 모멘텀 
대다수 시장 참여자들은 2026년 연말까지 LG에너지솔루션의 미국 ESS 60Gwh  규
모의 잔여 수주 달성 가시성에 집중한다. 해당 ESS 사업 수주 가이던스 관건은 시기
보다 방향성이다. 단, 당사가 주목하는 연말 모멘텀은 한국 정부향 제3차 ESS 중앙
계약시장 사업자 선정으로 판단한다. 3차 규모는 1.13Gw로 1&2차 계약 규모(합산 
1.13Gw) 대비 확대되었다. 또한 언론보도상 LG에너지솔루션은 해당 수주를 위한 핵
심 4대 소재 등 전 Value-chain 국산화를 선언한 바 있다. 해당 수주 확정시, 동사
를 비롯한 해당 Value-chain에 긍정적 모멘텀이 예상된다.

투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 51만원 제시   
26년 연중 지정학 Risk, 에너지 대전환 영향 등에 전력 인프라 수요는 꾸준할 전망
이다. 향후 국내 이차전지 산업은 ‘L’ Shaped Cycle 전망, 반면 동사는 스마트카 T사
향 원통형 전지 출하량 증가, ESS 제조업 이상으로 변모하는 과정으로 산업 평균 성
장률 Outperform 가능하다. 투자의견 Buy 유지, 적정주가는 추정치 상향에 51만원
을 제시한다.

Meritz Research  2026. 10. 15

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 510,000원 
현재주가 (10.14) 422,000원

Document 289: Eurozone_ Amid rising inflation, the business climate remains resilient, but consumer confidence falls back (2026-10-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Eurozone_ Amid rising inflation, the business climate remains resilient, but consumer confidence falls back (2026-10-02).pdf

### Page 1

September 2026: Marked improvement in
services business confidence
September 2026: Business climate in the
manufacturing sector comfortably in the
expansion zone for the 8th month in a row
September 2026: Strengthening inflationary
pressures
September 2026: Slight further increase in
supply tensions
September 2026: First decrease in 5 months of
household confidence
September 2026: Marked increase in inflation,
to a 3-year high
Eurozone: Amid rising inflation, the business climate remains resilient, but consumer

confidence falls back
02/10/2026 3
We have selected two inflation measures (with and without energy) and six survey indicators: 1/ & 2/ business confidence, as
measured by the PMIs in the manufacturing and services sectors; 3/ & 4/ input and output prices components of the
composite PMI (in order to identify direct inflationary pressures); 5/ the suppliers’ delivery times component of the
manufacturing PMI (a direct indicator of possible supply difficulties and supply-demand imbalance, and therefore, indirectly,

of inflationary pressures in the making); and 6/ consumer confidence (measured by the “assessment of financial situation in
the next 12 months” in order to capture the impact of inflation on purchasing power). The trends in each of these indicators
are observed relative to month m=0, corresponding to the start of the conflict. Each line does not represent the level of the
indicator, but its cumulative variation compared to month m=0.
The overall picture from the confidence surveys available for September is globally positive. According to the PMIs, the

business climate in services has indeed seen a clear improvement, and that in the manufacturing sector remains comfortably
in expansion territory for the 8 th month in a row. However, consumer confidence has recorded its first decline in 5 months,
likely driven by rising inflation and concerns over purchasing power. News on the inflation front is negative. PMI surveys
report a strengthening of inflationary pressures: this trend is not a surprise given the tensions in energy prices, but it is

nonetheless significant. As for inflation itself, it continues to rise, more markedly in September: +0.6 percentage points, to
3.8% year-on-year (Eurostat flash estimate), of which 0.4 pp du to the higher energy contribution. Inflation excluding energy
increased as well, by 0.2 pp to 2.3% (of which 0.1 pp due to the rise in food inflation).
Source: S&P Global, Eurostat, European Commission, Macrobond, BNP Paribas
ECONOMIC RESEARCH
Change since m = 0 = February 2026 / February 2022.

Unit: inflation change in percentage point, point for confidence surveys.
Eurozone: Inflation (incl. & excl. Energy)
Eurozone: Manufacturing PMI

delivery times

### Page 1

#consumer #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ household incomes, assets and savings
update - June quarter 2026
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
10:24 October 08 2026 Share
New Zealand incomes and savings are rising, as is the value of households’ financial
assets. However, the value of housing assets remains flat.
Key takeouts from Stats NZ’s latest update on household incomes, assets and savings (June quarter
2026)

### Page 2

Household incomes
Disposable income levels in the economy grew by 5.2% in the year to June, down from 6.5% in the
year to March

Savings and wealth
Household savings have continued to rise, climbing by $2.5b over the past quarter.

Overall household wealth levels were up 1.9% over the past year. Under the surface, the make-up of
household wealth continues to shift.

Interest costs 
Overall household income levels are continuing to rise. Much of that is due to increases in
entrepreneurial earnings (which is in part due to increased earnings in the agricultural sector). Wage
and salary growth has been more limited but has picked up.
Household savings levels have been rising for three years now, with another solid rise over the past
three months. 
Overall household wealth levels have risen modestly. Although the value of households’ financial
assets is continuing to rise, housing assets have essentially shown no growth since late 2023.
Household spending on interest costs rose just slightly in the June quarter. That’s the first increase in
two years. The easing in average borrowing costs seen over the past few years has come to a close
with the RBNZ’s hiking cycle now in train.
Underpinning the rise in overall income levels have been continued increases in entrepreneurial
earnings, which are up 12% over the past year. That will include stronger earnings in the agricultural
sector, which has been boosted by the strength in commodity export prices. That lift in agricultural
earnings has been reflected in stronger economic confidence and spending in many rural regions.
We expect commodity export earnings will remain firm over the year ahead. 
In contrast, wage and salary earnings are up just 2.7% over the past year. That’s up from 1.8% in the
year to March, but it’s a fairly muted rise, especially given the continued increases in the population
and inflation of 4.1%.
June’s large increase in savings builds on the solid increases seen over the past three years.
The savings rate in the June quarter was 3.7% of disposable incomes, down just slightly from last
quarter.
The value of households’ financial assets has continued to rise and is up 4.6% over the past year. 
However, the value of housing and land assets is down 0.9% over the past year, and it has remained
essentially unchanged since late 2023. We expect housing market conditions will remain soft over
the coming year, limiting increases in overall household wealth. 
Households’ spending on interest costs rose just slightly in the June quarter, climbing by 0.3%.
While that’s a relatively small change, it is the first increase that we’ve seen in two years.

### Page 3

Related articles

Media Contact:
Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist
P: +64 9 336 5668
M: +64 21 710 852
Over the past few years, many borrowers saw their debt servicing costs fall as they rolled off earlier
high mortgage rates and onto lower ones. That helped to support household spending.
However, the easing in average borrowing cost has now ended. Recent months have seen mortgage
rates pushing higher as the RBNZ has begun to hike the Official Cash Rate, with further hikes
expected over the coming year. 
Looking ahead, we don’t expect a significant increase in average household borrowing costs.
However, we won’t see the same support for spending that we did over the past few years.

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
TODAY’S INSIGHTS
Financial Markets
Markets moved lower overnight as mixed US mega-cap
earnings renewed concerns around the AI trade. A sharp rise
in oil prices following deadly attacks in Saudi Arabia added to
the risk-off tone, driving safe-haven demand for US Treasuries.

• US equities came under pressure as investors again
questioned the prospective returns from significant AI-
related capital spending and elevated valuations, while
ongoing hostilities in the Middle East further weighed on
sentiment. The S&P 500 fell 1.4%, while the NASDAQ led
declines, down 2.6%. The Dow Jones fared better, finishing
broadly unchanged, while the VIX rose 18.3%. European
equities also moved lower, with the Euro Stoxx 50 down
1.9% and the FTSE 100 declining 0.3%.

• Asian equities were again hit by concerns around the AI
trade, with mixed earnings from US mega-caps failing to
improve sentiment. Semiconductor stocks weighed heavily
on the KOSPI, which fell 3.5%, while the Hang Seng
declined 3.0% and the Nikkei 225 lost 2.7%. Locally, the
ASX 200 fell 0.4%.
• US Treasuries were the primary beneficiary as global risk
sentiment deteriorated. Safe-haven demand saw the 2-year
Treasury yield fall 16bps, while the 10-year yield declined

15bps. Futures markets have pared expectations for further
rate hikes in 2026, taking the implied year-end fed funds
rate to 4.09%. In Europe, the 10-year Bund yield fell 9bps
while the French 10-year government bond yield rose
6bps, widening the France-Germany spread further to 1.74
percentage points, among its widest levels since the euro
debt crisis.
• Australian government bond yields were little changed,
with the 3-year yield flat and the 10-year yield falling

3bps. Swap markets remain unconvinced of additional
RBA tightening in 2026, although the implied probability
of a November move remains elevated at 54% following
hawkish comments from RBA Deputy Governor Hauser on
Wednesday, taking the implied year-end cash rate to 4.81%.
• The US dollar lost some ground, with the DXY down 0.1%.
The AUD slipped 0.1% against the greenback to 0.7019,
while the euro also declined 0.1% and the sterling was
broadly unchanged. The yen drove the relative weakness in

the USD, gaining 0.8% on safe-haven demand.
• Oil prices rose sharply as deadly attacks in Saudi Arabia
heightened concerns around an escalation in the conflict.
President Trump claimed in a Truth Social post that Iran was
doing everything it could to drive oil prices higher ahead of
the US midterms. Brent climbed 4.0% to US$113/bbl, while
WTI gained 2.2% to US$99.3/bbl. Dated Brent traded at
US$124/bbl.
• Gold fell 2.7% amid higher oil prices and renewed inflation

concerns. Industrial metals were hit hard by weaker global
risk sentiment, with copper falling 1.9%, compounded by
its exposure to the AI trade, while aluminium declined 1.1%
and iron ore lost 1.3%.
International Data
US initial jobless claims fell from 200k to 194k last week,
indicative of little-to-no job shedding.
The US leading index edged up from -0.1% to 0.0%, pointing
to growth around trend over the coming 6 months.
The Kansas City Fed manufacturing index declined from 14

to 11 in October, though the current level is still well above the
average of the past 5 years.
Euro Area consumer confidence edged down from -16.8 to
-17.9 in October, holding modestly below the 5 year average of
-15.3.
Local Data
In New Zealand, CPI rose 0.7%qtr in Q3 (3.7%yr), while
measures of core inflation took a step higher (see here). Our
New Zealand team now expects the RBNZ to raise the OCR by
25bps to 3.00% at its October policy review (see here).

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

India: RBI monetary policy committee 
meeting (7-Oct)

The macro environment has undergone 
significant changes since the RBI 
monetary policy committee’s (MPC) 
August policy meeting, with global oil 
prices rising past $100bl, and US Fed 
kickstarting their rate hike cycle. 
Domestically, core and headline 
continued to rise, laying the ground for 
inflation to quicken beyond 5.8 -6.0% in 
the second half as price risks broaden. 
Strong 1QFY27 GDP growth meanwhile 
pointed to the economy’s relative 
resilience to external shocks.

Besides fuel feeding into food costs, 
weather has also proved to be disruptive, 
with indications that the El Niño 
phenomenon could extend into 2027. 
International food benchmarks, including 
the UN Food Price index and the 
Bloomberg spot agricultural indice s, 
continue to climb. India’s food inflation 
has become more broad -based, shifting 
beyond vegetables and pulses to sugar, 
milk, protein groups, and edible oils. Sub-
par weather has contributed to this 
upmove, besides idiosyncratic factors 
like swing in production trends (cobweb), 
diversion for fuel usage etc.

After two years of normal monsoon, 
cumulative southwest rainfall this year is 
set to end with rainfall around 12 -13% 
below the long -term average, the most 
since 2015 and resulting in a slower 
build-up in reservoir levels.  
Global benchmark oil price gauge has 
averaged ~$93pb in FY27 year -to-date, 
up 36% yoy  vs FY26. Retail pump fuel 
prices were adjusted in quick succession 
in May26 and have been left unchanged 
since then, while other fuel products 
including CNG, aviation fuel, LPG etc 
have continued to climb. Add to this, 
other pockets like telecom, restaura nts, 
business services, transport fares etc 
have been inching up, reflecting early 
signs of broadening in price pressures (on 
sequential basis). A gradual broadening 
of price pressures is likely to keep 
headline inflation high and narrow the 
real rate buffer significantly.

Authorities are also conducting liquidity 
absorption measures through market 
operations like VRRR, open market 
operations and sell-buy FX swaps. This is 
likely to align the overnight call money 
rates to the benchmark repo rate, after 
having fallen sharply d ue to the surplus 
in the banking system.  Liquidity and core 
inflation have historically displayed a 
modest positive relationship, as per our 
study. As such, the RBI's inflation 
mandate has likely reinforced the push to 
drain excess liquidity.

We expect the RBI MPC to raise the repo 
rate by 25bp to 5.5% , citing pipeline 
inflationary pressures. Stance could be 
changed to withdrawal of 
accommodation to signal hawkish intent. 
We expect one more hike within 4Q26, 
though the odds of additional hikes have 
risen as oil prices stay stubbornly high 
and weather remains unfavourable.

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 2, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Malaysia

We expect industrial production to grow 
by 5.8% yoy in August from 4.7% yoy in 
July. Strength in  export-oriented 
manufacturing is likely to persist , 
supported by sustained demand for 
artificial intelligence-related electronics 
goods, while mining output may have 
recovered from its contraction in July.

Taiwan

September trade and inflation data are 
forthcoming. Export growth is expected 
to sustain the 40% pace seen in the 
previous month. The global AI boom is 
facing rising headwinds from 
synchronised rate hikes by G3 central 
banks, higher bond yields, and rising  
financing costs. That said, there are so 
far no clear signs of a significant 
slowdown, based on the high -frequency 
indicators we track across AI demand, 
investment, and supply.

CPI is expected to rebound to 2.4% YoY 
in September, up from 2.0% in August. 
The earlier arrival of the Mid -Autumn 
Festival this year may have boosted food 
and services prices on a YoY basis. The 
rebound in global energy prices should 
also have lifted tran sport costs, 
including gasoline prices and airfares. 
While electricity tariffs will remain 
frozen, the December review implies a 
possibility of an electricity price hike 
after the local elections.

Given still -strong economic growth, 
persistent inflation, and the potential for 
inflation expectations to rise, we 
maintain our view that the central bank 
will raise the policy rate by a modest 
12.5bps to 2.125% in December.

Thailand

We expect Thailand’s headline inflation 
to accelerate to 3.2% yoy in September 
from 2.5% yoy  in August, slightly 
exceeding the central bank’s 1 -3% 
inflation target. Faster increases in 
energy and food prices likely pushed 
overall inflation higher, despite an 
easing in electricity tariffs starting 
September. We expect the Bank of 
Thailand (BOT), w hich views the rise in 
inflation as largely supply -driven, to 
keep its policy rate stable at 1.00% for 
the remainder of 2026, aiming to support 
an economy with low and uneven growth.

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

Bank of Thailand (BOT) (October 28)

We expect the BOT to maintain its policy 
rate at 1.00% at its 28 October meeting, 
extending its pause for a fourth 
consecutive decision as it balances 
inflationary pressures against uneven 
and subdued economic growth. Our view 
is aligned with fixed market income 
pricing. Headline inflation rose anew to 
2.8% yoy in September 2026, driven by 
higher energy, food, and core price 
increases, but remained within the upper 
end of the central bank’s 1 -3% target 
range. As the BOT is still likely to look 
through what it views as temporary, 
supply-driven inflationary pressu res, it 
will likely refrain from tightening policy, 
and keep interest rates accommodative 
to support the soft and uneven growth 
cycle.

European Central Bank (October 29)

We expect the ECB to extend its pause, 
keeping the deposit facility rate at 2.5%. 
Market participants will instead focus on 
the central bank’s assessment of rising 
French and Italian bond yields and 
widening spreads versus Bunds. While 
we do not expect dir ect references to 
individual countries, the ECB is likely to 
signal that recent developments in 
France reflect the country’s weaker 
fiscal and debt profile rather than a 
broader systemic concern. Policymakers 
will seek to differentiate between a 
localised bond sell -off and a Eurozone -
wide market disruption, justifying their 
decision to monitor developments rather 
than implement specific measures at this 
juncture. Meanwhile, inflation continues 
to rise, with little relief expected in 
upcoming readings given elevated 
global fuel prices. 
Bank of Japan (October 30)

The Bank of Japan is expected to keep 
the overnight call rate unchanged at 1.25% 
at this meeting, pausing after the 25bp 
hike in September. The BOJ is likely to 
assess the impact of the previous rate 
hike before taking further action. The 
recent stabilisat ion of the yen also 
reduces the urgency of another hike at 
this meeting.

The tightening bias should remain intact. 
Growth and inflation remain broadly on 
track, leaving room for the BOJ to revise 
up its FY2026 –FY2027 GDP and CPI 
forecasts. Wage growth has also 
remained strong, with Rengo reportedly 
targeting wage increases of 6% in 2027, 
reinforcing the BOJ’s confidence that 
underlying inflation can be sustained 
around its 2% target. We continue to 
expect the policy rate to rise to 1.50% in 
December 2026 or January 2027, 
followed by a further increase to 1.75% 
by 2Q 2027.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Hong Kong SAR

Export growth is expected to accelerate 
from 53.0% yoy in August to 55.0% in 
September, in line with the pickup in 
China’s export growth from 25.0% to 
36.3%. As a key re-export hub for China,

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 23, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

Hong Kong should benefit from robust 
AI-related electronics trade, with 
electrical machinery, equipment and 
parts exports to Hong Kong accounting 
for 20.6% of China’s total exports. 
Import growth is also expected to remain 
firm, supported by resilient expo rt 
orders and gradually improving 
investment sentiment, sustaining 
demand for intermediate and capital 
goods.

Singapore

Singapore’s industrial production (IP) 
likely remained in expansion for a 13th 
consecutive month in September 2026, 
although we expect growth to moderate 
to 7.6% yoy from August’s strong 15.4% 
yoy pace. IP growth continued to be 
underpinned by the electronics and 
precision engineering clusters, which 
has been key drivers of Singapore’s 
manufacturing strength, reflecting 
sustained demand for artificial 
intelligence-related hardware. However, 
performance across clusters remained 
uneven, with chemicals and biomedical 
manufacturing likely contracting, and 
tempering overall gains.

South Korea

The preliminary estimate for 3Q26 GDP 
is likely to show growth remaining solid 
at 3.2% yoy , slightly below 3.7% in 2Q 
and 3.8% in 1Q. Nominal GDP growth is 
likely to remain in double digits, 
significantly outpacing real GDP growth, 
thanks to improved terms of trade amid 
rising memory export prices. By 
expenditure, exports picked up in 3Q in 
both nominal and real terms, supported 
by stronger semiconductor shipments. 
Investment also appeared robust, as 
reflected in the equipment investment 
estimation index and machinery order 
index. Private consumption, however, 
appeared to weaken amid the sharp 
KOSPI correction since July following  
the introduction of leveraged ETF 
regulations. Our full -year GDP growth 
forecasts of 3.5% for 2026 and 2.4% for 
2027 remain on track.

Taiwan

The preliminary estimate for 3Q GDP is 
likely to show growth remaining strong 
at 12 –13% yoy , close to 12.9% in 2Q, 
albeit below the peak of 15.4% in 1Q. 
Exports maintained strong double -digit 
growth in 3Q (44.6% yoy  in nominal 
terms and 21.1% in real terms), supported 
by robust shipments of semiconductors, 
servers and other AI -related products. 
Machinery investment also sustained 
strong double-digit growth, underpinned 
by higher capacity utilisation in the ICT 
sector and a resulting increase in capital 
goods imports. Private consumption 
appeared to have strengthened further, 
supported by stock market gains, 
improving wage growth, and the 
associated wealth and income effects. 
Construction investment remained the 
weak s pot, weighed down by elevated 
inventories in the new-home market and 
the ongoing need for inventory digestion. 
We maintain our 2026 and 2027 GDP 
growth forecasts at 11.6% and 5.6%, 
respectively, with risks tilted to the 
upside.

Document 397: United States _ A shock-resistant expansion (2026-09-24)

Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-24

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026

world
A SHOCK-RESISTANT EXPANSION
US growth remains resilient despite successive shocks. According to our forecasts, it is expected to reach 2.2% in 2026 and
2.3% in 2027. Its main driver is still investment in artificial intelligence (AI), which has remained entirely unaffected by the
energy crisis. The labour market has seen a marked improvement in job creation and a fall in the unemployment rate. Core

inflation remains stubbornly high and above the Federal Reserve (Fed)’s target. Against this backdrop, the Fed has begun a
hiking cycle in September, which, according to our scenario, would bring the Fed Funds target to 4.50% (upper limit) by Q1
2027. At the same time, long-term rates are under upward pressure.
1  This includes: energy and communications; data centres (facilities); IT equipment and peripherals; communication equipment (equipment); software; and research and develop-

ment (intellectual-property products).
THE ENERGY SHOCK IS NOT HOLDING BACK GROWTH
Driven by AI-related investment, US growth is holding up despite ri -
sing energy inflation. We forecast GDP growth at 2.2% in 2026 and
2.3% in 2027 on an annual average basis. The AI-related investment
cycle1 (+14.8% y/y in H1 2026) is expected to continue, accompanied by
potential productivity gains. However, other private-investment seg -
ments (including residential property) remain sluggish. In addition, the

rising cost of capital could accentuate its ‘K-shaped’ profile. Household
consumption is expected, on the whole, to benefit from the rebound in
job creation and to remain above 2% y/y through to the end of 2027.
In H1 2026, it held up against resurging inflation due to an average
year-on-year increase of 11.5% in tax refunds and a savings rate stan-
ding at its lowest level since 2022 (2.8% in Q2).
RECORD CAPITAL-RAISING DRIVEN BY AI
This year, AI-related investments are driving massive capital-raising

by companies through loans, bond issues or share placements. This
trend is set to continue given the seemingly colossal scale of the fun -
ding requirements, even as risk premia in the bond market begin to
widen. According to their statements, the five tech giants could invest
nearly USD 900 bn this year and over USD 1,000 bn next year—more
than double the figure for 2025. Spurred on by regulatory easing, the
major US banks have announced that they will mobilise significant re-

sources to support projects considered strategic for the United States
(AI, defence, energy and infrastructure). While funding for the AI boom
appears unaffected by rising long-term interest rates, these latter are
likely to continue to weigh on household demand for mortgages. Since
the start of the conflict in the Middle East, 30-year mortgage rates
have risen by 78 bp (to 6.76% at the start of September). Conversely,
consumer lending remained buoyant in H1 2026 and is supporting

household spending.
TWO-SPEED GROWTH IN INDUSTRY
Industrial production rebounded slightly in 2025 (+1.1%), driven by AI
and aerospace. We forecast an acceleration to +1.5% in 2026 and +2%
in 2027. Investment in AI is bolstering the electronics sector, while the
(civil and military) aerospace sector is rebounding as order backlogs
are cleared and defence requirements rise. Conversely, sectors ex -
posed to rising tariffs (in particular, the steel, aluminium and auto -

motive sectors) are still on the back burner, contrary to the objective
of these measures. This positive momentum in industrial production,
while concentrated, is expected to continue, bolstered by the recove -
ry in business sentiment. The ISM Manufacturing Index has been in
expansionary territory since early 2026, despite ongoing pressures on
prices and supply chains.

world
LABOUR MARKET: A LOW UNEMPLOYMENT RATE ON A DOWNWARD
TREND
Job creation (non-farm payrolls) has rebounded, averaging +80,000 per
month since the start of the year, compared with +10,000 in 2025. This
growth is no longer limited to the healthcare sector. Excluding health-
care, job creation is up by an average of +38,000 in 2026, compared

with -47,000 in 2025. Manufacturing and construction, two sectors
driven by AI, are contributing to this rebound. At the same time, the
labour force has not grown since January 2025 due to the decline in the
foreign-born workforce (down 4.2% cumulatively over the period). Ex -
cluding the impact of COVID-19, the labour force participation rate is at
its lowest level since the 1970s, standing at around 61.5%. The rebound
in labour demand and persistent pressure on the labour supply have

brought the unemployment rate down to 4.1% in August 2026 (a 0.3 pp
fall since the start of the year). The jobless rate is expected to fall to
3.9% at the end of 2027 (4.2% corresponds to full employment, accor -
ding to the CBO) thanks to a job creation rate of around 40,000–60,000
per month.
INFLATION: STRUCTURALLY ABOVE TARGET
The rise in oil prices has triggered a rebound in CPI inflation. The
contribution from the energy component (+1.2 pp) largely accounts for

the rise in inflation since February. A slowdown was seen (from +4.2%
to +3.4% y/y) between the May peak and August. This is expected to
continue due to base effects on energy prices. We anticipate the trough
to be +1.8% y/y in Q2 2027 before a stabilisation at around +2.3% y/y
later in the year.
Meanwhile, core inflation is expected to remain above the Fed’s 2%
target, and above +2.5% y/y in 2027. This highlights the structural
nature of inflation, beyond short-term fluctuations linked to energy.

Non-housing services are expected to show significant inertia, with in-
flation hovering at around +3% y/y by the end of 2027. The potential for
disinflation also appears limited for housing-related services, as the
rise in rents shows no signs of abating. Finally, inflation on non-energy
goods is slowing as the effects of tariffs fade. However, the AI cycle is
exerting upward pressure through the general rise in the cost of elec -
tronic goods.
FED: GREEN LIGHT FOR RATE HIKES

The FOMC has embarked on a cycle of rate increases. The Fed Funds
target was raised to 4.0% (+25 bp) at the September meeting, and we
anticipate two further rises that would bring the policy rate to 4.5% in
January 2027. These three rises (made possible by reassuring develop-
ments in employment and economic activity) are intended to reverse
the three cuts in 2025 adopted to counter the ‘downside risks’ to the
labour market at that time. As a result, the committee can recalibrate

its policy towards ‘price stability’ and, through its interest rate deci -
sions, reflect the priority given to this objective in its communications.
The risks point towards further monetary tightening: in our baseline
scenario, core inflation would remain sticky and the fall in unemploy -
ment would give the FOMC room for manoeuvre to raise rates further.
PUBLIC FINANCES: UNDERLYING DETERIORATION AND PRESSURE ON
LONG- TERM RATES
According to our estimates, the federal deficit is set to widen by 0.7 pp

to -6.6% of GDP in 2026 (by 0.4 pp to -3.1% for the primary deficit),
weighed down by falling tariff revenue (refunds of tariffs already col -
lected and lower-than-expected receipts). In 2027, the end of this one-
off effect is expected to lead to a marginal improvement to -6.3% (to
-2.8% for the primary deficit). The growing burden of interest payments
(3.5% of GDP in 2026 and 3.6% in 2027, following 3.2% in 2025) and
the conflict in Iran (affecting the military budget) are weighing on the

budget balance and federal-debt dynamics. Federal debt is still on an
upward trend, with no particular change in direction, exceeding 100% of
GDP (101.9%, up 3.3 pp, in 2026, and 103.5% in 2027). It is expected to
reach 110% by the end of the decade against a backdrop of budget de-
ficits of close to 6% of GDP , assuming fiscal policy remains unchanged.
Furthermore, the mid-term elections (which see the entire House of
Representatives and one-third of the Senate up for re-election) could

result in a divided Congress or in both chambers changing hands to
the Democratic Party. Should the former occur, this would increase the
risk of a legislative deadlock, while the presidential veto would limit
the Democrats’ ability to influence fiscal policy in the latter scenario.
Against this backdrop, pressure on the bond market is likely to continue
in the coming months. Treasury issuance is rising, fuelled by increa -
sing spending requirements (in areas such as AI, defence and supply

chains). At the same time, shifts in investor demographics are increa-
sing demand volatility, and issuances by companies in the artificial
intelligence sector are intensifying competition for capital. We antici -
pate a 10-year yield of 4.95% in Q1 2027, before a slight easing brought
about by Fed rate rises, which would lend credibility to the price-sta -
bility objective. However, according to our forecast, 10-year yield is
expected to remain close to a 20-year high, at 4.80% in Q4 2027.

THE US RELIES ON IMPORTS FOR ITS GROWTH
The US trade deficit has recently started to rise again. According to
our forecasts, it is expected to account for the same proportion of GDP
in 2026 as it did in 2024 (4.4%). This trend is driven by imports of
AI-related capital goods, which are set to rise from 1.2% to 2.1% of GDP
between 2022 and 2026. Investment in AI is, in fact, import-intensive
(Chart 2), which dampens its positive impact on growth and highlights

the US’s dependence on foreign supplies in a cycle that it is neverthe-
less spearheading.
The additional tariffs have failed to reverse the trend in the trade de -
ficit. Furthermore, the IEEPA tariffs struck down by the Supreme Court
were replaced, in July 2026, by ‘Section 301’ tariffs (forced labour) ran-
ging from 10% to 12.5%. These tariffs target 60 countries and have
been introduced on top of existing sector-specific tariffs (steel, alumi -

nium and automotive). Following these adjustments, the statutory rate
(measured against the 2024 import structure) remained slightly above
10%. The main risks relate to potential new sector-specific tariffs and
the renegotiation of the USMCA.

Document 415: What Will Warsh Do_

Source PDF: all report pdf/What Will Warsh Do_.pdf
